# Skills · P · part 187

1,072 entries, principle-define-errors-away to principle-never-block-on-the-human. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **principle-define-errors-away**: Remove error conditions by redesigning the API instead of handling them. Use when designing an API's error surface, when error handling in a module is getting dense, or when the user asks to "simplify error handling" or "reduce exceptions". ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-define-errors-away))
- **principle-define-the-domain-first**: Use when a new noun, state, or stored field appears, one word is doing two jobs, or two sources use different words for what may be one thing. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-define-the-domain-first))
- **principle-define-the-question**: Apply at the start of an analysis, before writing the first query. Write down the decision the analysis serves, the metric definition, the population, the time window, and the result that would change the decision. Without this, analysis drifts into fishing. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-define-the-question) · ★1)
- **principle-delegate**: How to spawn and trust subagents. Use before delegating work, and when a subagent's report comes back. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-delegate) · ★1)
- **principle-delegate-after-the-contract**: Assess delegation when work could use separate contexts, parallel execution, or a less capable model. ([BumpyClock/agent-templates](https://github.com/BumpyClock/agent-templates/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack/principle-delegate-after-the-contract) · ★2)
- **principle-delegate-down-a-tier**: Guidance for choosing useful delegation and an appropriate model. Read when independent or parallel work could help. ([sjdonado/dotfiles](https://github.com/sjdonado/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/modes/sjdonado-mode/skills/principle-delegate-down-a-tier) · ★16)
- **principle-delegate-implementation-review-stays-here**: Apply when handing work to a subagent. Implementation delegates; design, review, verification, and git mutations stay with the lead. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-delegate-implementation-review-stays-here))
- **principle-deliver-and-prove**: Establish readiness to deliver an implementation and verify its expected behavior before editing. Use when beginning implementation or when new findings change what delivery or proof requires. ([ahmadabdalla/aasystem](https://github.com/ahmadabdalla/aasystem/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deliver-and-prove) · ★1)
- **principle-depend-on-caller-contracts**: Apply when you design a dependency or interface, or call through a collaborator to reach another object. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-depend-on-caller-contracts) · 3 copies)
- **principle-dependencies**: Dependencies that point one way, onto the language's own tools and stable public APIs, one home for each library, the runtimes a project targets, floating ranges with a pinned lockfile, and deciding logic kept pure. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-dependencies))
- **principle-dependency-inversion**: Use when business logic creates, imports, or knows about infrastructure — databases, HTTP clients, frameworks, file formats — when core code cannot be unit-tested without external services or heavy mocking, when deciding where an interface should live or which direction a dependency should point, or when layering policy against mechanism, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. High-level policy should not depend on low-level details; both depend on abstractions owned by the policy. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-dependency-inversion))
- **principle-derive-dont-sync**: Apply when about to store a value that could be computed from existing state, or to keep two copies of one truth in step: derive from one source instead. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-derive-dont-sync) · ★1)
- **principle-derive-from-one-owner**: WHEN the same fact or decision is computed or stored in more than one place; NOT for modelling legal states or collapsing pass-through layers; gives it one owner that other sites derive from or receive. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-derive-from-one-owner) · ★37)
- **principle-derive-state-never-duplicate-it**: Apply when designing stateful systems, caches or UI stores. Keep one source of truth and compute everything else from it; a second copy of the same fact drifts. ([Michael-Scrumlord/dzstack](https://github.com/Michael-Scrumlord/dzstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-derive-state-never-duplicate-it))
- **principle-describe-interfaces-first**: Describe the caller contract before creating or materially changing a public, cross-module, or non-obvious interface. Use when callers need explicit guarantees, failure behavior, effects, or invariants to use the interface correctly. Skip trivial private helpers and contracts already obvious from established types and conventions; use the authoritative source for generated interfaces. ([kriscard/Kriscard-Stack](https://github.com/kriscard/Kriscard-Stack/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/principle-describe-interfaces-first))
- **principle-design**: A design built from proven patterns and SOLID objects that put behaviour with the data it serves, short because it fits the problem. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-design))
- **principle-design-first**: Use when a change adds shared state, a new module, adapter, tool or script, a flow across screens, or a new requirement that breaks a rule of the current design. Use it also when the request says to write the code now. Decide and review the code design before the code. ([binhle312/cty1ng](https://github.com/binhle312/cty1ng/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-first))
- **principle-design-for-concurrency**: Use when actors already overlap: two workers, retries, callbacks, background tasks, or late results can touch the same resource. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-design-for-concurrency))
- **principle-design-for-failure**: Use when a call, job, or state change has an effect someone else can see and it can time out, be retried, partly succeed, or end without you knowing. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-design-for-failure))
- **principle-design-for-operations**: Keeps software correct and identical wherever it runs: crash-only design (fails, stops halfway, runs twice, runs concurrently), idempotent jobs, append-only data runs, and twelve-factor config, logs and releases. Use when writing jobs, pipelines, ETL, workers, CLIs, retries, config, env vars, secrets, logging, deployment or releases. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-for-operations) · ★1)
- **principle-design-for-throughput**: Apply when designing, implementing or reviewing NeoHaskell framework data structures, service flows, concurrency, storage, codecs or generated APIs. Design toward a hypothetical 50,000 requests per second with explicit cost and workload assumptions; distinguish design reasoning from measured performance. ([neohaskell/NeoHaskell](https://github.com/neohaskell/NeoHaskell/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-for-throughput) · ★348)
- **principle-design-foundations**: Resolve competing design criteria from the current use case, trusted values, ownership, and concrete costs rather than a fixed architecture. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-design-foundations) · ★1)
- **principle-design-harness**: AI エージェントに UI を作らせても品質がぶれないよう、制約・文脈・検証・フィードバックの 4 層で作業環境を整えるための原則。こぎそ氏が提唱した「デザインハーネス」に基づく。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-harness) · ★10)
- **principle-design-patterns**: Design patterns — Gang of Four and beyond. Strategy, factory method, observer, decorator, adapter, facade, template method, command, repository, dependency injection. Auto-load when designing class structure, refactoring toward a pattern, or evaluating whether a pattern applies. ([aibot88/sec_skill_store](https://github.com/aibot88/sec_skill_store/tree/HEAD/skills/claudskills/principle-design-patterns) · ★6)
- **principle-design-patterns**: Design patterns — Gang of Four and beyond. Strategy, factory method, observer, adapter, template method, dependency injection. Auto-load when designing class structure, or evaluating whether a pattern applies. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-patterns) · ★3)
- **principle-design-system**: デザインシステムを作る、直す、使うときに、それぞれの要素がどうあるべきかを判断するための原則。基本原則、部品とパターン、トークン、運用の観点。Alla Kholmatova『Design Systems』をはじめ、広く参照される文献に基づく。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-design-system) · ★10)
- **principle-design-system-first**: Apply to any UI work. Start from the app's existing design system, tokens, and primitives; a new primitive needs justification. Sweat transitions, alignment, and error states; every feature earns its place. ([RestartDK/dotfiles](https://github.com/RestartDK/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/agents/skills/dstack/principle-design-system-first) · ★1)
- **principle-design-the-core**: Encodes rules in the shape of the data and keeps them in a pure core: sum types, state machines, tables, value objects, parsing at the edge, I/O in thin adapters, ports only where something really varies. Use when choosing types, schemas or data structures, placing new logic, or wiring a database, API, CLI, queue or LLM client. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-the-core) · ★1)
- **principle-design-to-the-port-not-the-vendor**: Apply when writing or changing a port's contract, adding a product or a delivery mechanism behind one, naming a config table or glossary term after a product, or writing code that branches on which product or source is in use. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-design-to-the-port-not-the-vendor))
- **principle-deterministic-tests**: Apply to any test that uses time, randomness, ordering, network, concurrency, or asynchronous UI. The same code and the same inputs must give the same result. Wait on conditions. Do not use fixed sleeps. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-deterministic-tests) · ★1)
- **principle-deterministic-tests**: Tests that each fail when, and only when, the behaviour they guard breaks. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-deterministic-tests))
- **principle-discomfort-is-a-signal**: Apply when the named constraint feels easy or flattering, or when the owner tours opportunities and activity lists. Prefer the uncomfortable answer. Activity lists are the default dodge. ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-discomfort-is-a-signal) · ★6)
- **principle-distributed-systems**: Distributed systems principles — CAP, consistency models (linearizable, causal, eventual, read-your-writes), quorum, leader election, partitioning, idempotency across nodes, fallacies of distributed computing. Auto-load when reasoning about CAP/PACELC trade-offs, choosing a consistency model, ordering events with logical clocks, distinguishing exactly-once delivery from exactly-once effects, designing replication or partitioning strategy, or assessing distributed failure modes. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-distributed-systems) · ★3)
- **principle-divide-the-work**: Divide the work when a task splits into independent parts, such as several files, sources, or checks whose results do not depend on each other, or when one step would flood the main thread with bulk output. Give each part to its own subagent and merge short summaries. ([ChandlerWeeks/chandler-skills](https://github.com/ChandlerWeeks/chandler-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-divide-the-work))
- **principle-do-not-block-the-human**: Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct after the fact; reserve confirmation for irreversible actions. ([fabiommendes/codehood](https://github.com/fabiommendes/codehood/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-do-not-block-the-human) · 3 copies)
- **principle-do-not-block-the-human**: Aplique quando tiver a tentação de perguntar 'devo fazer X?' em trabalho reversível. Prossiga, apresente o resultado e deixe o humano corrigir o rumo depois; reserve a confirmação para ações irreversíveis. ([fabiommendes/skills](https://github.com/fabiommendes/skills/tree/HEAD/pt-BR/principles/principle-do-not-block-the-human))
- **principle-documentation**: ドキュメントに何を載せ、何を載せないかの原則と事例。リポジトリのドキュメント、エージェント向けの指示ファイル、コードコメントを書くとき、見直すときに使う。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-documentation) · ★10)
- **principle-documentation-and-adrs**: Records architecture decisions (ADRs) and the docs around a change. Use when making a decision that's expensive to reverse, changing a public API, shipping user-facing behaviour, or updating a README, changelog, or rules file. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-documentation-and-adrs) · 2 copies)
- **principle-domain-types-over-primitives**: Use when meaning travels in bare primitives, strings, dicts, or tuples — parameters of the same primitive type getting swapped, validation or unit interpretation repeated at call sites, ids, money, emails, or ranges passed as raw values — when designing function signatures or domain models, when asked how to stop type-confusion or stringly-typed bugs, or when deciding whether a value deserves its own type, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. Wrap domain values in named types; parse once at the boundary, then trust the type. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-domain-types-over-primitives))
- **principle-dont-invent-problems**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage decides whether a concern is a problem, whether to classify it, or how sure to be. Do not invent a problem the input does not support. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-dont-invent-problems) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dont-solve-yet**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage is about to diagnose, brainstorm, or recommend. Stop once the problem is identified. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-dont-solve-yet) · 2 copies)
- **principle-doubt-driven-development**: Subjects non-trivial decisions to a fresh-context adversarial review before they stand. Use before an architectural decision under uncertainty or a non-trivial commit, when asserting a property you can't prove (safe, idempotent, scales, matches the spec), when working in unfamiliar code, or when the stakes are high or irreversible (auth, security, migrations, deploys). ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-doubt-driven-development) · 2 copies)
- **principle-dry**: 识别并消除知识重复，确保系统中每条知识有唯一权威表示。当需要重构代码、整理文档、优化配置、建立单一数据源时使用。覆盖代码重复、数据冗余、文档重复三大维度，提供具体消除策略和权衡建议。 ([tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge](https://github.com/tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge/tree/HEAD/AGENTIC_SPEC_FORGE/spec_stage_skill/common_normal/principle-dry) · ★5)
- **principle-dry**: One home for every piece of knowledge. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-dry))
- **principle-durable-loop**: Apply when the outcome should continue with no person feeding the next task. Build the access first. ([jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack](https://github.com/jl-cmd-projects/DeepStack/tree/HEAD/plugins/deepstack/skills/principle-durable-loop))
- **principle-early-return**: Apply when writing or reviewing a function with nested conditionals, an else after a return, or a main path buried inside if blocks. Put each case that ends the function in a guard clause at the top that exits at once, and leave the main path last at the outermost indentation level. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-early-return))
- **principle-earn-every-element**: WHEN adding UI controls, settings, options, API parameters or document sections; NOT observing a user journey or weighing scope trade-offs; keeps only elements with a stated user need. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-earn-every-element) · ★37)
- **principle-eliminate-avoidable-error-states**: Use when a design would add an exception, error result, retry branch, or caller obligation for a condition that could be normal, idempotent, or impossible by construction. Test semantics and invariants before making callers handle it; retain explicit failures when callers genuinely need the distinction. ([kriscard/Kriscard-Stack](https://github.com/kriscard/Kriscard-Stack/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/principle-eliminate-avoidable-error-states))
- **principle-encode-corrections**: Make recurring corrections durable in tests, checks, scripts, or structure. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-encode-corrections))
- **principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**: Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text. - **principle-encode-over-document**: When a documented rule has been violated twice, stop strengthening the documentation and encode the rule as a type, helper, or tool-checked structure. Use when deciding where a new rule should live, when a review finds a repeat regression, or when a checklist keeps growing instead of getting sharper. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-encode-over-document) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-encode-the-procedure**: When you do a project procedure a second time, or the user corrects how you did it, propose or update a project skill. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/lab/skills/principle-encode-the-procedure))
- **principle-encode-the-rule**: Encode a repeated instruction as a check, not more prose. Used by kevbot when a lesson would otherwise stay in chat. ([kevherro/kevbot](https://github.com/kevherro/kevbot/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-encode-the-rule))
- **principle-enforce-access-explicitly**: Apply to any data read or write path. CRUD, field-level security, and sharing are deliberate and stated: with sharing on the class, WITH USER_MODE or Security.stripInaccessible() on the data path, and a written reason for every without sharing or system-mode exception. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-enforce-access-explicitly) · ★1)
- **principle-enforce-distributed-authority**: Prevent stale or competing workers from committing protected changes through atomic claims, conditional writes, and fencing. Mandatory when work can move between workers, leases expire, leadership changes, or failover can leave an earlier actor running. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-enforce-distributed-authority) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-enforce-idempotent-effects**: Make equivalent requests converge on one logical effect through receiver-side identity and atomic arbitration. Mandatory when a command or external side effect may be delivered or invoked more than once. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-enforce-idempotent-effects) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-enforce-where-it-is-stored**: Apply when more than one client (a web app, an API caller, an agent or MCP tool, an import job) can write the same data, or when a client fills in or corrects a field before sending it. The system that stores the data enforces its rules for every writer; a client that covers for a missing server rule hides the gap. ([quotegoat-ux/bleat](https://github.com/quotegoat-ux/bleat/tree/HEAD/plugins/bleat/skills/principle-enforce-where-it-is-stored))
- **principle-engagement-is-not-consent**: Apply when a signal such as a like, comment, follow or visit is used as a reason to reach out or to keep data. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-engagement-is-not-consent) · ★3)
- **principle-engineer-tdd**: Strictly enforce outside-in TDD on every implementation task. Acceptance test first from the issue's EARS/Gherkin scenarios; modules grown via one-behavior RED → GREEN → REFACTOR loops with fake adapters at seams; real adapters earn contract tests; wiring proved by the acceptance test going green. Each step is its own commit per the caller's commit-message template. Encodes scaffold-vs-production boundaries, mandatory edge-case coverage, banned test anti-patterns, and the iron rules. ([MartinKChen/harness-claude-code](https://github.com/MartinKChen/harness-claude-code/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-engineer-tdd))
- **principle-error-design**: Apply when adding or changing an error: its type, message, public shape, status code or retry guidance. Say what failed and why, end on a way forward, carry typed context, keep reassurance provable, and translate each failure deliberately at the boundary. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-error-design))
- **principle-error-handling**: Error handling principles — errors as values vs exceptions, wrapping and context chains, retry with exponential backoff and jitter, circuit breakers and bulkheads, log-once boundary discipline. Auto-load when discussing errors as values, retry, exponential backoff, jitter, fail fast, fail soft, idempotent retry, error wrapping, or deadlines. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-error-handling) · ★3)
- **principle-event-driven**: Event-driven architecture — event sourcing, CQRS, sagas, schema evolution, consumer groups, partitions, idempotent handlers, outbox pattern, dead letter queues. Auto-load when designing event-driven systems, evaluating event sourcing or CQRS, planning saga workflows, implementing idempotent consumers or the outbox pattern, managing dead letter queues, or assessing whether event-driven architecture fits the problem. ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-event-driven) · ★3)
- **principle-everything-as-code**: Apply when Azure, AWS, Terraform or pipeline resources change, including a portal or console edit. Codify the change in the repository and import any drift so the next plan matches what is running. ([JayCommit/jstack](https://github.com/JayCommit/jstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-everything-as-code) · ★1)
- **principle-evidence**: 技术信号、笔记经验或模型判断不一致，或者需要提供引用时使用。核对代码事实与实际读取的原文，避免把推断写成信号。 ([aicoder2048/pi-super-agent](https://github.com/aicoder2048/pi-super-agent/tree/HEAD/docs/modules/examples/stock-ta/skills/principle-evidence))
- **principle-evidence-before-verdicts**: Apply when investigating, reviewing, or reporting conclusions. Support verdicts with inspected evidence and distinguish defects from uncertainty and preferences. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-before-verdicts))
- **principle-evidence-follows-the-commit**: Keep verification evidence bound to the exact pull-request revision when a readiness claim depends on a head SHA, base SHA, lower stack layer, or expected evidence set, especially after any of those bindings change. ([kriscard/Kriscard-Stack](https://github.com/kriscard/Kriscard-Stack/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/principle-evidence-follows-the-commit))
- **principle-evidence-in-every-claim**: Apply to every report, reply, PR body, and review. Each claim carries its evidence or its label (measured, inferred, or guess) in the same sentence. Never report a check you did not run, and never link something you did not open. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-in-every-claim))
- **principle-evidence-not-claims**: Apply before you report any test status, coverage, fix, or readiness. A verdict must rest on an executed run against the artifact under test, quoted and rerunnable. Proxies such as collect-only, compiling, a dry run, or green CI on another commit are NOT RUN. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-evidence-not-claims) · ★1)
- **principle-evidence-over-assertion**: Not a workflow; read when a step names it. Ground claims in evidence gathered now, and check load-bearing runtime claims against telemetry. Read before stating a fact about behavior, impact, frequency, or coverage. ([sjdonado/dotfiles](https://github.com/sjdonado/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/modes/sjdonado-mode/skills/principle-evidence-over-assertion) · ★16)
- **principle-evidence-over-report**: When a report and the repository disagree — a worker claims success with no commit, a tracker says done but the gate is red — the repo is right and the report is narrative. Apply whenever consuming status from tickets, workers, comments, dashboards, or pipeline state. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-evidence-over-report) · ★3)
- **principle-evolve-and-restore-state**: Keep durable data, messages, work, authority, and external effects safe across mixed-version deployment, rollback, migration, failover, backup, and restore. Mandatory when changing a durable representation or protocol, running mixed versions, resuming old work, or designing rollback, migration, backup, restore, or failover. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-evolve-and-restore-state) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-example-adoption**: Make runnable examples useful to application developers. Mandatory when creating, changing, reviewing, or diagnosing anything under examples/. ([ElmoJones3/archer](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/archer/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-example-adoption) · ★1)
- **principle-excuse-first**: Apply before writing any outreach. Every message needs an honest, recent, relevant reason to exist. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-excuse-first) · ★3)
- **principle-exhaust-design-space**: Apply when a novel interaction or architecture decision has no useful precedent. Build two or three competing prototypes and compare them before committing. ([ImAno177/cstack](https://github.com/ImAno177/cstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-exhaust-design-space))
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing. ([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★20k · 10k installs · 1k copies)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins) · ★17k · 5.4k installs)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. - **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Use for a UI, architecture, or product choice the codebase has never faced, or one decided by how it feels in use. Build two or three rival throwaway prototypes and pick based on comparing them side by side. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Build a few materially different cheap prototypes and compare evidence before committing to one design. Use when for novel interactions or architecture with no strong precedent. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★117 · 4 copies)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★38)
- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: WHEN a novel interaction or architecture choice has material uncertainty; NOT for settled mechanical changes; compares a few distinct cheap alternatives before commitment. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★37)

- **principle-exhaust-the-design-space**: Gated principle: apply on net-new UI surfaces, material layout redesigns, greenfield architecture, or when explicitly requested — never on routine bugfix tickets. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space) · ★20 · 7 copies)

([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-experience-first) · ★20k · 10k installs · 1k copies)
- **principle-experience-first**: Apply when product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs come up. Choose user delight over implementation convenience; ship fewer polished features over more rough ones. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins) · ★17k · 5.3k installs)
- **principle-experience-first**: Apply when product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs come up. Choose user delight over implementation convenience; ship fewer polished features over more rough ones. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-experience-first**: Apply when product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs come up. Choose user delight over implementation convenience; ship fewer polished features over more rough ones. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-experience-first**: Use on tradeoffs in product, UX, API ergonomics, or feature scope. When the convenient build and the better result for users point different ways, choose the better result. Ship fewer capabilities that are complete rather than many that are partial. - **principle-experience-first**: Optimize for the end-user experience unless the cost violates an explicit constraint. Use when product, UX, API design, or feature-scope tradeoffs arise. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-experience-first) · ★117 · 4 copies)
- **principle-experience-first**: Apply when product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs come up. Choose user delight over implementation convenience; ship fewer polished features over more rough ones. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-experience-first) · ★37)

Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-explain-the-number-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-explicit-backend-no-silent-placement**: Use when changing numerical backends, execution placement, fallbacks, or transformation support. ([xshi19/normix](https://github.com/xshi19/normix/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-explicit-backend-no-silent-placement))
- **principle-explicit-dependencies**: Architecture. Apply when a component acquires collaborators or capabilities (clock, fs, network, storage, other services), when choosing a DI mechanism, or when a test reaches for monkeypatching. ([Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config](https://github.com/Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config/tree/HEAD/dot_claude/skills/principle-explicit-dependencies))
- **principle-explicit-errors**: Apply whenever code handles an exception (catch, suppress, translate, retry), decides to skip, drop, truncate, or stop early on data (an empty parse, a missing lookup, an unpriced row, a lookback or coverage cap, a loop that reads only the first source), or writes the text of an error, failure, or rejection message that a person or agent will read to fix the problem. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-explicit-errors) · ★2)
- **principle-exploratory-vs-confirmatory**: Apply when planning or reporting an analysis. Pre-state hypotheses and the analysis plan, and label anything found after looking as post hoc. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-exploratory-vs-confirmatory) · ★1)
- **principle-express-intent**: Apply when authoring or reviewing how code, tests, documentation, comments, commit messages, or skills express the purpose, behavior, constraints, and reasoning behind work. ([pghk/dotfiles](https://github.com/pghk/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/source/private_dot_config/agents/shared/skills/principles/principle-express-intent) · ★2)
- **principle-extend-at-evidenced-seams**: Apply when you add a required variant or decide whether code needs an extension point. Base the choice on known requirements or a concrete testing need. ([nilpath/nilpath-marketplace](https://github.com/nilpath/nilpath-marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/plugins/nstack/skills/principle-extend-at-evidenced-seams) · 3 copies)
- **principle-extract**: 从已完成的决策中提炼可复用原则，写入原则库。 当用户拍板后、决策分析结束、或用户说"这个经验值得记住"时触发。 ([picsky/flowos](https://github.com/picsky/flowos/tree/HEAD/templates/skills/principle-extract))
- **principle-facts-over-judgment**: Apply when writing any analysis, summary, or recommendation. Keep facts, assumptions, judgments, and predictions on separate lines, each labeled. ([frinfo702/cowork](https://github.com/frinfo702/cowork/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-facts-over-judgment))
- **principle-fail-fast**: Code that fails where a wrong state is first detected, with an error that says how to recover, and holds an expected absence in its type. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-fail-fast))
- **principle-fail-fast**: Apply when code meets a state it cannot handle: a broken invariant, a missing or bad config value, an unexpected variant, or a failure you are tempted to default, retry, or log past. Stop at that point with an error that says what broke and where, and catch failures in one top-level handler instead of along the way. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-fail-fast))
- **principle-fail-loud**: 例外を握りつぶす、既定値で埋める、フォールバックで続ける、「たぶん大丈夫」で進めそうなときに適用する。見過ごしを許さず、エラーを出して止める。 ([B16B1RD/bb-writ](https://github.com/B16B1RD/bb-writ/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fail-loud))
- **principle-fail-loud-and-fast**: Apply when writing error handling, API boundaries or fallbacks. Surface failures where they happen with full context; never swallow an exception or substitute a silent default. ([Michael-Scrumlord/dzstack](https://github.com/Michael-Scrumlord/dzstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fail-loud-and-fast))
- **principle-fail-loud-on-invalid-domain**: Use when handling invalid parameters, failed solves, infinities, clamps, or numerical thresholds. ([xshi19/normix](https://github.com/xshi19/normix/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fail-loud-on-invalid-domain))
- **principle-failing-test-first**: Apply before writing production code. Write the failing check first, at the fastest level that expresses the behavior, and watch it fail for the right reason. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-failing-test-first))
- **principle-falsify-or-slogan**: Apply when accepting a nominated constraint. Require what would prove it wrong within a fixed window (metric, queue, cycle time, cash, conversion, blocked dependency). ([Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates](https://github.com/Shixuuu/grokbot-pi-templates/tree/HEAD/templates/community/research-briefings/first-principles-t5qkap/skills/principle-falsify-or-slogan) · ★6)
- **principle-fast-tests**: Apply when writing, reviewing or speeding up slow tests. A test runs under 1 second and a service's suite under 5 minutes, and speed comes from the test's shape, never from lower fidelity. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fast-tests))
- **principle-feature-branch-isolation**: Apply before starting any change. Work happens on daryl/&lt;kebab-words&gt; in its own worktree, never on main, never in the primary worktree. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-feature-branch-isolation))
- **principle-fewest-moving-parts**: Apply when choosing a design or an implementation. Pick the least fancy, simplest, most robust option with the least logic that still scales. ([Torus403/skills](https://github.com/Torus403/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fewest-moving-parts))
- **principle-finish-or-report**: Apply at the end of any run. Never deliver a partial silently. Either the whole thing is done, or say precisely what is left and why. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-finish-or-report))
- **principle-finish-the-behavioral-path**: Apply when a change crosses producers, consumers, persistence, or delivery paths. Complete and verify the connected work needed for the caller to experience the requested result. ([alexph/brunel](https://github.com/alexph/brunel/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-finish-the-behavioral-path))
- **principle-finish-the-migration**: Apply to internal migrations without concrete external or persisted compatibility requirements. Migrate every in-scope caller and remove the old contract. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-finish-the-migration) · 2 copies)
- **principle-first-learning**: 当需要学习新领域或技能以解决特定问题时 ([DracoUnion/dankoe-skills](https://github.com/DracoUnion/dankoe-skills/tree/HEAD/principle-first-learning) · ★14)
- **principle-fix-root-cause**: Use when you fix a bug or a failure, when review fixes repeat, or when several paths enforce one rule. Find the first wrong decision. ([binhle312/cty1ng](https://github.com/binhle312/cty1ng/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-cause))
- **principle-fix-root-cause**: Use when something is broken and the cause is unknown ([sansernonline/SQT-Marketplace](https://github.com/sansernonline/SQT-Marketplace/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/e-commerce/.agents/skills/principle-fix-root-cause) · 25 copies)
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. - **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to the first incorrect operation and fix its cause. A guard is appropriate when it handles a legitimate state. ([yohannesakd/ticketkit](https://github.com/yohannesakd/ticketkit/tree/HEAD/bundle/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: Apply when debugging a bug, test failure, crash, or unexpected behavior. Reproduce first, trace the symptom back to the first place the system went wrong, and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes**: 调试时使用。把每个症状追回到系统第一次走错的地方，并在那里修好。守卫只有在它处理的状态是合法的时候才算修复。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-fix-root-causes))
- **principle-fix-root-causes-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fix-root-causes-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-fix-the-bar-first**: WHEN comparing, racing, iterating on or reviewing candidates, existing or new; NOT for a gate that has already failed or for choosing which checks to run; records the selection criteria before evaluation and holds them after results. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-fix-the-bar-first) · ★37)
- **principle-fix-the-latent-issue**: Apply when a request or visible defect may be a downstream symptom. Choose the right problem from the user goal, then fix its causal mechanism. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-fix-the-latent-issue) · 2 copies)
- **principle-fixes-need-a-yes**: Apply when you see a mechanical error in the user's draft: citation format, unit format, a typo. List it; change the file only after the user accepts that item. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fixes-need-a-yes) · ★1)
- **principle-flag-your-own-corrections**: Apply whenever you are about to admit fault in any wording — a fact, number, claim, or check you already stated turns out to be wrong, vacuous, premature, or unverified. Say so explicitly rather than quietly citing the corrected value, and treat the admission itself as a reflect trigger. Auto-fires because no regex can enumerate the ways a model admits error. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-flag-your-own-corrections) · ★2)
- **principle-flaky-is-a-bug**: Apply to any intermittent result: a test that passes and fails on the same code, a retry that changed red to green, or a suite that fails only in CI. Find the source of the nondeterminism. Retries and longer timeouts hide it. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-flaky-is-a-bug) · ★1)
- **principle-flat-methods**: Use when a method or function's control flow nests — if/elif/else chains, arrow-shaped or pyramid code, loops wrapping branches — when refactoring toward early returns or guard clauses, when deciding how to structure conditional logic, or when a change means adding one more arm to a deep branch, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. No else and no elif — guard the edges and keep the happy path flat on the left margin. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-flat-methods))
- **principle-fluid-interfaces**: Make gesture-driven and interactive UI feel physical: direct manipulation, interruptibility, springs, velocity handoff, momentum, rubber-banding, and spatial consistency. Use when building or reviewing drag, swipe, sheet, or any touch interaction. ([NickDabizaz/skills](https://github.com/NickDabizaz/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fluid-interfaces))
- **principle-follow-official-docs**: Apply when the project uses a third-party library, platform, or service. Follow one option its current official docs prescribe, and cite that page in the PR. ([anas-yousef/pstack-grok](https://github.com/anas-yousef/pstack-grok/tree/HEAD/.cursor/skills/principle-follow-official-docs))
- **principle-follow-the-existing-pattern**: Apply before naming, placing or shaping anything new in code. Find the nearest existing analogue and copy its pattern instead of inventing a variant. ([geovane-gc/claude-harness](https://github.com/geovane-gc/claude-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-follow-the-existing-pattern))
- **principle-formatting**: Use when writing Markdown in an Obsidian vault: creating or editing any note. ([GoBeromsu/secondbrain-skills](https://github.com/GoBeromsu/secondbrain-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-formatting) · ★1)
- **principle-formulation**: Formulate candidate principles from P2+ patterns and distinguish novelty from known laws. ([rmcveyhsawaknow/my_hinters](https://github.com/rmcveyhsawaknow/my_hinters/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-formulation) · 2 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. ([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★20k · 10k installs · 1k copies)

- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Use before writing logic, picking core types, or deciding whether infrastructure or features come first. Settle data shapes and groundwork first, and isolate any state that concurrent actors could change. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Choose the core data structures, ownership, and sequencing first so downstream logic becomes simpler. Use when before writing logic, especially when state or concurrency is involved. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★117 · 4 copies)
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking) · ★38)

- **principle-foundational-thinking**: 로직을 쓰기 전에 적용한다. 핵심 타입과 자료구조를 고르고, 뼈대 작업과 기능 작업의 순서를 정하고, 동시에 움직이는 주체들이 무엇을 공유하는지 묻는다. 자료구조를 제대로 잡으면 그 뒤 코드는 자명해진다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: ロジックを書く前に適用する。コアとなる型とデータ構造の選択、足場と機能の作業順序の決定、並行するアクターが何を共有するかの問い。データ構造を正しくして、下流のコードが自明になるようにする。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: For the Box kitchen project only. Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. ([shouteck/omakase-box](https://github.com/shouteck/omakase-box/tree/HEAD/.devin/plugins/box-pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-foundational-thinking. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: Apply before writing logic: choosing core data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-foundational-thinking))
- **principle-foundational-thinking**: 写逻辑之前使用：选定核心类型和数据结构，安排脚手架与功能的顺序，问并发参与者共享什么。把数据结构定对，后面的代码就会变得显然。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-foundational-thinking))

- **principle-frame-follows-the-data**: Apply when setting up a figure: choose a closed box or an open L-frame from the data, take line widths, marker sizes and fonts from the preset, and keep to 3 to 5 major ticks. Override the preset only for a stated reason. ([taixuann/rstack](https://github.com/taixuann/rstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-frame-follows-the-data) · ★1)
- **principle-freeze-the-grid**: 기존 영상이나 cue에 붙은 음악의 시간 계약(beat, t0, meter, 첫 다운비트, hit 번호)을 지키고, 바꿔야 할 때는 다시 만들 범위와 비용을 먼저 밝히는 판단 원칙. ([ynginkim/op55](https://github.com/ynginkim/op55/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-freeze-the-grid))
- **principle-fresh-eyes-find-bugs**: Apply when reviewing issue bodies, scenarios, or findings. The author stops seeing their own mistakes; a reviewer with no prior context catches them. Fresh context is mandatory when a spawn tool exists, and self-review must be labeled as such. ([jirayusueb/qa-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/qa-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-fresh-eyes-find-bugs))
- **principle-freshness**: 解释行情时间、时效敏感信号、缺少信号或缓存不新时使用。区分数据时间与发现时间，并保留延迟和未知范围。 ([aicoder2048/pi-super-agent](https://github.com/aicoder2048/pi-super-agent/tree/HEAD/docs/modules/examples/stock-ta/skills/principle-freshness))
- **principle-frontend**: Routes frontend engineering through rendering, ownership, reuse, state, CSS, and performance decisions. Use when building or reorganizing a React/Vue/Svelte UI ("프론트엔드 구조 잡아줘"); choosing a React + Vite or Next.js shell, folder/public-API, or server/client boundary; improving component reuse or state ownership; selecting CSS Modules/Tailwind/CSS-in-JS and token structure; or setting dependency, bundle, and CSS performance strategy. Not for material visual/UX judgment or DESIGN.md — use design; public API/server contracts — use api/principle-backend; TypeScript-only work — use principle-program… ([GoBeromsu/craft-skills](https://github.com/GoBeromsu/craft-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-frontend))
- **principle-frontend-ui-engineering**: Builds and reviews user-facing UI that is accessible, responsive, and visibly designed. Use when building or styling pages and components, choosing a visual direction, managing UI state, or reviewing a UI or its accessibility. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/output/claude/skills/principle-frontend-ui-engineering) · 2 copies)
- **principle-frontmatter**: A seven-line principle whose body starts on line 5. ([EvanBatten/pstack-for-cc](https://github.com/EvanBatten/pstack-for-cc/tree/HEAD/eval/parity/fixtures/skills/principle-frontmatter) · ★2)
- **principle-functional-composition**: Apply whenever writing or reshaping code, in any language. Build behavior from small pure functions composed into pipelines, keep effects at the edges, and write entry points as short orchestrators of reusable modules. ([ja153903/jimmy](https://github.com/ja153903/jimmy/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-functional-composition) · ★1)
- **principle-functional-core**: Apply when writing business logic, or a change that mixes decisions with I/O. Decisions live in pure functions, and a thin shell does the I/O. ([pdevito3/pdstack](https://github.com/pdevito3/pdstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/pdstack/skills/principle-functional-core))
- **principle-functional-programming**: 関数型プログラミングの考え方でコードを設計・実装・レビューするスキル。業務のモデルを型で表し、純粋関数による状態の遷移として処理を組み立て、I/O を端に寄せ、依存はカリー化で注入する。 TypeScript / JavaScript で新しい処理を設計・実装するとき、ドメインモデルや業務フローを型と関数で表したいとき、副作用と純粋なロジックの分け方や依存注入の方法に迷ったとき、既存コードをテストしやすい形に整理したいとき、関数型の観点でコードをレビューしてほしいときは必ずこのスキルを使うこと。 「関数型で書いて」「FP で」「純粋関数にして」「副作用を分けて」「DI どうする？」「ドメインモデルを設計して」といった依頼でも使う。 ([kazukit-dev/dotfiles](https://github.com/kazukit-dev/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/skills/principle-functional-programming))
- **principle-functions-do-one-thing**: Apply when writing or reviewing a function with mixed abstraction levels, behavior-selecting flags, unclear arguments, hidden effects, or hidden call-order requirements. It gives each function one job with explicit inputs and effects while preserving required atomic operations. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-functions-do-one-thing))
- **principle-gaps-are-not-passes**: WHEN a check was skipped, unavailable, timed out or returned no result before a verdict; NOT for choosing which checks to run; reports each gap and gives a verified, not verified or inconclusive verdict. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-gaps-are-not-passes) · ★37)
- **principle-gate-before-release**: Apply when asked whether a release ships. No verdict without the checklist: blockers closed or deferrable by name, regressions green, scenarios passing, flakes triaged, every claim cited. The verdict is one word first. ([jirayusueb/qa-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/qa-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-gate-before-release))
- **principle-gate-before-spend**: Apply when paying for the rest of a plan: a review's later stages, a fan-out's next wave, or a whole-file re-run. Decide with the evidence in hand and spend only what the decision needs. ([jirayusueb/egm-stack](https://github.com/jirayusueb/egm-stack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-gate-before-spend))
- **principle-generalize-from-rejection**: Apply when a mechanical gate — a lint, validator, blocked-command guard, or CI check — rejects your output for the same reason twice in one session. Fix the generation pattern producing the violation, not just the specific instance. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-generalize-from-rejection) · ★2)
- **principle-give-responsibilities-a-home**: Apply when policy decisions, mutable state, or invariants are duplicated or passed through several layers. Identify the owner and keep enforcement and updates with the responsibility they serve. ([alexph/brunel](https://github.com/alexph/brunel/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-give-responsibilities-a-home))
- **principle-goal-driven-execution**: Defines success criteria that can pass or fail, and loops until they are verified. Use when starting multi-step work, when a request has more than one reading, and before declaring work done. ([jiyeol-lee/dotfiles](https://github.com/jiyeol-lee/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/principle-goal-driven-execution))
- **principle-golang-respect-context**: Respect request lifetimes when implementing or reviewing Go operations that accept, propagate, or retain a context. Covers cancellation before work and through dependent calls, including context-bearing wrappers. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-golang-respect-context) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-graceful-degradation**: Classify dependency failures and fallbacks while preserving user work, required safeguards and honest outcomes. ([sjdonado/dotfiles](https://github.com/sjdonado/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/modes/sjdonado-mode/skills/principle-graceful-degradation) · ★16)
- **principle-grade-the-evidence**: Before you pass on any claim, give it one grade from the strength and independence of its evidence, never from venue, citation count, or repetition. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/lab/skills/principle-grade-the-evidence))
- **principle-guard-the-context**: Use when a step needs bulk reading (many files, long logs, large diffs) or hands work to another stage: send the reading to subagents, keep summaries and paths in the main thread, and hand off through files. ([fabricio21x/fbs](https://github.com/fabricio21x/fbs/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-guard-the-context) · ★1)
- **principle-guard-the-context-window**: Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads. - **principle-guard-the-context-window**: Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)

Use when when reads, logs, fan-out, or generated output threatens to dominate context. - **principle-guide**: Guide decision-making using principles and trade-off analysis frameworks. Use when making technical decisions, evaluating alternatives, designing solutions, or choosing between options. Helps establish lasting principles. ([krhrtky/dotfiles](https://github.com/krhrtky/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/claude/skills/principle-guide))
- **principle-handle-message-uncertainty**: Preserve honest outcomes when requests, replies, acknowledgements, timeouts, or cancellation can be delayed, lost, duplicated, or reordered. Mandatory when code must decide what a missing, late, repeated, or out-of-order request, reply, acknowledgement, timeout, or cancellation means. ([ElmoJones3/bootes](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/bootes/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-handle-message-uncertainty) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-hide-information**: Use when deciding what a module's interface should reveal, when callers must know an implementation decision — data format, storage backend, call ordering, concurrency strategy — to use the module, when one design change would force edits in many modules, or when choosing what to keep out of an API, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. Hide each decision likely to change inside one module; let the interface expose outcomes, never decisions. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-hide-information))
- **principle-hide-representations**: WHEN a private wire, storage or framework representation crosses a module boundary; NOT for internal types that never leave their module; exposes domain concepts and keeps special-case policy at the edge. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-hide-representations) · ★37)
- **principle-hide-useful-complexity**: Apply when designing modules, interfaces, or abstractions. Hide cohesive complexity behind a small interface that reduces caller knowledge. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-hide-useful-complexity))
- **principle-hold-the-scope**: Use when a new request arrives, a design or plan comes back from someone else, or work is about to be called done. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-hold-the-scope))
- **principle-honest**: Tell the user when they are wrong. Use whenever the user states a belief, makes or proposes a decision, shares a plan or draft, asks for approval or feedback, or when what they ask for conflicts with the evidence. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-honest) · ★1)
- **principle-human-gates**: Fire human stops for the profile. agentic: product CLARIFY + final package (review+G3+diff). full: also G1 and G2. Use at handoffs. ADR-002, ADR-003. ([theb4w/sdd-starter](https://github.com/theb4w/sdd-starter/tree/HEAD/.agent/skills/principle-human-gates))
- **principle-human-on-the-loop**: Work autonomously and loop the user in only where the work needs their judgment. Use whenever you run a workflow on the user's behalf. ([andrezzoid/newsroom](https://github.com/andrezzoid/newsroom/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-human-on-the-loop))
- **principle-humans-approve**: Apply before anything reaches a prospect or changes the brain's context. ([ycanerden/gluely](https://github.com/ycanerden/gluely/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-humans-approve) · ★3)
- **principle-i18n**: Internationalization (i18n) & localization (l10n) principles — locale-aware date, time, time-zone correctness (persist UTC in DB, render local), CLDR plural rules, message catalogs (ICU MessageFormat, gettext), translatable string composition (no concatenation), bidirectional & right-to-left layout. Auto-load when adding a new locale, formatting dates/times/numbers/currency for display, persisting timestamps, building message catalogs, composing user-facing strings from variables, working with right-to-left scripts, choosing between backend and frontend locale negotiation, or auditing UI-stri… ([lugassawan/swe-workbench](https://github.com/lugassawan/swe-workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-i18n) · ★3)
- **principle-idempotent-automation**: Apply when writing or rerunning Terraform, scripts, GitHub Actions or Azure DevOps pipelines. A second run with the same inputs must succeed, change nothing further and be safe to start twice. ([JayCommit/jstack](https://github.com/JayCommit/jstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-idempotent-automation) · ★1)
- **principle-immutable-values-for-math-objects**: Use when designing mathematical values, fitting results, or reusable numerical artifacts. ([xshi19/normix](https://github.com/xshi19/normix/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-immutable-values-for-math-objects))
- **principle-importance-is-obvious**: Apply when building or changing any UI. Make what matters, what happened, and what to do next obvious without explanation. ([tommyc10/tblock](https://github.com/tommyc10/tblock/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-importance-is-obvious))
- **principle-incremental-implementation**: Delivers a change in thin vertical slices, each green and committed before the next. Use when implementing a change that spans more than one file, picking up the next task from a plan, landing unfinished work behind a feature flag, or when a change feels too big for one step. ([joseiciano/dotfiles](https://github.com/joseiciano/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai-clis/global-ai/setup/skills-setup/skills/principle-incremental-implementation) · 2 copies)
- **principle-independent-verdict**: Only a verdict from a context that did not write the code counts — an author checks what it meant to build, not what it built, and an author-verified claim on its own diff is not enough. Apply before trusting any review, CI-green diff, self-certification, or self-verified work on a nontrivial change, including verdicts carried across rebases where the patch may have drifted. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-independent-verdict) · ★3)
- **principle-inference**: Rules for model state, serving semantics, prefix reuse, and speculative inference. ([para1lel/sglang.cpp](https://github.com/para1lel/sglang.cpp/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-inference))
- **principle-information-architecture**: How to decide a web app's navigational structure — page hierarchy, where a sub-view lives, how nav is grouped, and how users keep their bearings. The design step that creates structure, upstream of the heuristic audit that checks it. Use when adding a page, deciding tabs-vs-routes, grouping a nav menu, introducing a new section or audience, or reviewing whether a growing area still has a usable map. ([oskarc/AssetHub](https://github.com/oskarc/AssetHub/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-information-architecture) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-integrate-through-review**: Apply when delivering implementation work or deciding whether it is complete. Integrate changes through PRs with central checks and independent review rather than equating local verification with delivery. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-integrate-through-review))
- **principle-intent-and-contracts**: Expose concrete meaning and constraints in names, types, outcomes, values, effects, and documentation; distinguish naming from behavior changes. ([leesangb/agents](https://github.com/leesangb/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/sb/skills/principle-intent-and-contracts) · ★1)
- **principle-intent-ladder-stop**: Apply when changing product behaviour or presentation, when purpose or contracts are missing, or when Open product questions is non-empty. If the intent ladder does not answer, stop. Do not invent product rules. ([hembrow-innovations/agent-core](https://github.com/hembrow-innovations/agent-core/tree/HEAD/stacks/heio-stack/skills/principle-intent-ladder-stop))
- **principle-interface-segregation**: Use when implementers stub out, throw on, or no-op methods they do not need, when an interface or base class has grown fat, when a change for one client forces recompiles or retesting of unrelated clients, or when designing interfaces shared by different client roles, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. No client should be forced to depend on methods it does not use. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-interface-segregation))
- **principle-interpret-observations**: Preserve evidence meaning when receiving observations, selecting current execution evidence, or calculating operational reports, rates, and coverage. Use for reporting correctness; does not grant authority to settle work or change recovery behavior. ([ElmoJones3/opinionated](https://github.com/ElmoJones3/opinionated/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/principle-interpret-observations) · ★1)
- **principle-invest**: 评估用户故事质量的六维度框架（独立性、可协商、有价值、可估算、小粒度、可测试），系统化审查需求质量。当需要编写用户故事、评审需求文档、拆分史诗故事时使用。提供分级检查清单和低质量故事的改进建议。 ([tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge](https://github.com/tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge/tree/HEAD/AGENTIC_SPEC_FORGE/spec_stage_skill/common_normal/principle-invest) · ★5)
- **principle-invest**: The quality bar for user stories — INVEST, vertical slicing, and the 3 Cs — plus the anti-patterns to reject. Other story-time skills apply it. Use when judging whether a story is well formed, or when a story-time skill points here. ([Team-Teddy-Development/story-time](https://github.com/Team-Teddy-Development/story-time/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-invest))
- **principle-invest-in-design**: Decide how much design effort an approved feature or fix warrants while it is being delivered. Use when the immediate patch raises a concrete question about contracts, state, dependencies, ownership, or structural friction in the affected responsibility; this principle does not grant authority to restructure. ([kriscard/Kriscard-Stack](https://github.com/kriscard/Kriscard-Stack/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/principle-invest-in-design))
- **principle-investigate-read-only**: WHEN the ask is why, how, review, render, recall or diagnose; NOT for requests that also authorise the change; delivers findings and leaves the subject under study unchanged. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-investigate-read-only) · ★37)
- **principle-isolate-mutable-state**: Apply before writing or delegating work when actors may share files, branches, runtime resources, or other mutable state. Separate independent writers before coordinating unavoidable shared writes. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-isolate-mutable-state))
- **principle-isolate-test-data**: Apply to any test that reads or writes persistent state: databases, queues, files, accounts, caches, third-party sandboxes. Each test must create the data it needs, use identifiers that nobody else uses, and leave nothing that changes the result of another test. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-isolate-test-data) · ★1)
- **principle-judge-with-fresh-eyes**: WHEN a quality or acceptance verdict on finished work is due; NOT for routine self-verification while building; has a context that did not build the work give the verdict, or runs a clean review pass from the inventory and states the limitation. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-judge-with-fresh-eyes) · ★37)
- **principle-justify-the-complexity**: Use when adding or keeping a layer, abstraction, option, mechanism, or second path, or when sequencing a refactor or rewrite. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-justify-the-complexity))
- **principle-keep-gates-traceable**: Apply when proposing blockers, planning work, or reporting completion. Require only gates traceable to accepted requirements or demonstrated safety dependencies. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-gates-traceable))
- **principle-keep-it-simple**: KISS, YAGNI and obvious code: decides whether a feature, option, flag, layer, dependency or abstraction should exist, presents the minimal version first, keeps what stays readable, and lands each change as if the design had planned for it. Use when proposing, designing, scoping, writing, changing or reviewing code, plans or specs. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-it-simple) · ★1)
- **principle-keep-one-source-of-truth**: Apply when you are about to create a second place that holds a fact: a config key or mapping, a cache or on-disk format, a document that describes what code or tests already say, or a field a tool will hold for you. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-one-source-of-truth))
- **principle-keep-secrets-out**: WHEN writing reports, logs, issues, commits, docs, wiki pages or memory, quoting terminal output, or sending data to another system; NOT for refusing authorised credential setup or personal data the task needs; keeps credential values and unneeded personal data out. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-keep-secrets-out) · ★37)
- **principle-keep-the-core-pure**: WHEN domain logic lands in an adapter, handler, job or UI component; NOT for wiring that makes no domain decision; keeps decisions in pure functions behind thin framework wiring. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-keep-the-core-pure) · ★37)
- **principle-keep-to-the-card**: Apply when the change grows past what the card asked: an extra hardening, a new binding, a side behavior change, a review finding that widens the scope, or a restore that turns into a redesign. The pull request does what the card asks; everything else becomes a proposed follow-up. ([geovane-gc/claude-harness](https://github.com/geovane-gc/claude-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principles/principle-keep-to-the-card))
- **principle-keep-tracking-aligned**: Apply when starting, switching, handing off, or completing tracked work. Keep authoritative workflow state, ownership, and relationships aligned with actual progress. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-keep-tracking-aligned))
- **principle-keep-uncertain-decisions-reversible**: Apply when an uncertain structural choice could be expensive to undo, such as an integration boundary, persisted representation, or public contract. Localize the uncertain decision and preserve a practical replacement path without speculative flexibility. ([alexph/brunel](https://github.com/alexph/brunel/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-keep-uncertain-decisions-reversible))
- **principle-kiss**: 保持设计和实现的简单性，识别并消除不必要的复杂度。当需要架构设计评审、代码简化重构、接口设计优化时使用。覆盖代码、架构、接口、流程四大维度，提供简单性检查清单和过度复杂性的诊断修复方法。 ([tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge](https://github.com/tikazyq/agentic-spec-forge/tree/HEAD/AGENTIC_SPEC_FORGE/spec_stage_skill/common_normal/principle-kiss) · ★5)
- **principle-knowledge-notes**: Decides what kind of knowledge note something is and what shape it takes: Term, Concept, Guide or Entity (person, organization, place, paper, book, media, product), with a template for each, plus claims within a page and evidence kept in Raw. Use for page-type decisions or corrections (is this a Term or a Concept, is this channel an Entity, which template does a book note use) and whenever a page is written from a template. A folder for an optional Entity type is never created without the owner's approval. Creating the page belongs to ingest. ([GoBeromsu/secondbrain-skills](https://github.com/GoBeromsu/secondbrain-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-knowledge-notes) · ★1)
- **principle-label-claims**: Labels every claim by how it is known: measured, inferred, or guess. Use when writing a reply, report, PR description, or review that states facts about code, behavior, or results. ([jiyeol-lee/dotfiles](https://github.com/jiyeol-lee/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/principle-label-claims))
- **principle-label-the-evidence**: WHEN writing findings, reports, handovers or verification summaries; NOT for deciding which checks to run; marks each claim's epistemic status, evidence level and source locator. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-label-the-evidence) · ★37)
- **principle-label-the-unknowns**: Apply when requirements, plans, test cases, or reports contain gaps, assumptions, or open questions. Every unknown must have a type, a state, an owner, and what it blocks. A bare TBD is not allowed. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-label-the-unknowns) · ★1)
- **principle-law-of-demeter**: Use when callers chain through several objects to reach what they need — a.b.c.d, foo().bar().baz(), or deep key paths like cfg["db"]["pool"]["size"] — when a change to one link's structure keeps breaking distant callers, when reviewing train-wreck expressions, or when deciding where a traversal should live, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. Talk only to your immediate friends — ask the neighbor for the outcome, never reach through it. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-law-of-demeter))
- **principle-layout**: A folder and file layout that shows the design, and one order inside every file. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-layout))
- **principle-laziness**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or when considering adding abstractions, layers or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([alexph/brunel](https://github.com/alexph/brunel/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-laziness))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★20k · 10k installs · 1.1k copies)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins) · ★17k · 5.5k installs)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 10k installs)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply while you restructure code, decide how large a diff should be, or feel the pull toward a new abstraction, an extra layer, or a parameter passed through many functions. Prefer deleting code, and ship the narrowest diff that fixes what was asked. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that fully solves the problem. Use when considering adding code, abstractions, dependencies, or speculative features. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★117 · 4 copies)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply to every change: when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, signal threading, or anything the user did not ask for (extra UI states, colours, copy, defaults, wider filters). Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([vvedantb/eva](https://github.com/vvedantb/eva/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★101)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★38)
