- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★20 · 7 copies)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion, Occam's Razor (fewest entities), and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([Scino/fstack](https://github.com/Scino/fstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-laziness-protocol) · ★13)
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: pstack, typed-only. Load only when the user names this skill or a loaded pstack skill routes to it. - **principle-laziness-protocol**: Stosuj przy sizingu diffa, refaktorze albo pokusie nowej warstwy. Najmniejsza zmiana i kasowanie przed dodawaniem. EN - laziness protocol, smallest diff, prefer deletion. ([Prezentytu/fiziyo-admin-portal](https://github.com/Prezentytu/fiziyo-admin-portal/tree/HEAD/.ai/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: 리팩터링하거나, diff 크기를 평가하거나, 추상화·계층·신호 전달 경로를 더하고 싶어질 때 적용한다. 삭제와 문제를 푸는 가장 작은 변경 쪽으로 기운다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: リファクタリング時、差分の大きさを評価するとき、または抽象化、層、シグナルの引き回しを追加したくなったときに適用する。削除と、問題を解決する最小の変更に傾く。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: For the Box kitchen project only. Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([shouteck/omakase-box](https://github.com/shouteck/omakase-box/tree/HEAD/.devin/plugins/box-pstack/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-laziness-protocol. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Writing code is cheap for you, which makes over-engineering easy. Counter it by borrowing a human maintainer's fatigue. Aim for the most result with the least code and complexity. ([thnkbig/skills](https://github.com/thnkbig/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when writing new code, refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add an abstraction, a dependency, a layer, or signal threading. Take the first rung of the reuse ladder that fully solves the problem, finish every place the change must reach, and never cut the safety floor. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: Apply when refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add an abstraction, a layer, or a new argument threaded through several files. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([yestheboxer/jig](https://github.com/yestheboxer/jig/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol**: 重构、评估 diff 大小，或想加抽象、分层、信号穿线时使用。偏向删除，以及能解决问题的最小改动。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-laziness-protocol))
- **principle-laziness-protocol-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem. ([evoclock/pi-poteto-mode](https://github.com/evoclock/pi-poteto-mode/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-laziness-protocol-pi) · ★9)
- **principle-lead-with-the-answer**: WHEN explaining, answering a question, or reporting a result, status or incident; NOT for putting a choice to the user or compressing someone else's text; puts the answer or decision in the first sentence and the background after. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-lead-with-the-answer) · ★37)
- **principle-lean**: Keeps changes small. Use before adding tests, CI, comments, error handling, fallbacks, retries, config options, dependencies or abstractions, and when reviewing a diff that has more of any of these than the task needed. ([gmdkaio/kstack](https://github.com/gmdkaio/kstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-lean) · ★1)
- **principle-lean-by-design**: Use when invoked by the user ([isthatcentered/dotfiles](https://github.com/isthatcentered/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/home/skills/skills/principle-lean-by-design) · ★1)
- **principle-least-privilege**: Apply when granting IAM, Azure RBAC or pipeline access for Azure, AWS, Terraform, GitHub Actions or Azure DevOps. Give each identity only the actions and scope the job needs, including GITHUB_TOKEN permissions and service connection scope. ([JayCommit/jstack](https://github.com/JayCommit/jstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-least-privilege) · ★1)
- **principle-lessons-to-skill**: 发现自己第二次写同一指令，或注意到反复出现的纠正时应用。把规则编码成 lint、元数据标志、运行时检查或脚本，而非每次在会话中重复使用类似的指令修复重复的问题。 ([feiandre/HiSkills](https://github.com/feiandre/HiSkills/tree/HEAD/skills/common-skills/principle-skills/principle-lessons-to-skill))
- **principle-let-models-judge**: Splits LLM work from code: the model judges fuzzy input, code checks the answer's structure; prompts teach the class, never the case; a wrong model answer is fixed with prompt, more reasoning or another model, not like a code bug; evals run rarely. Use for any AI engineering work: code that calls an LLM, prompts, agents, tools, model choice, evals. ([Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills](https://github.com/Med-Karim-Ben-Boubaker/karim-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-let-models-judge) · ★1)
- **principle-lifecycle-tasks-cancel-on-reentry**: Apply when a state machine's enter or lifecycle hook spawns a task (listener, subscription, async work) and can re-run for the same state: cancel the previous entry's task, in the library when you can. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-lifecycle-tasks-cancel-on-reentry) · ★1)
- **principle-liskov-substitution**: Use when a subclass refuses or weakens part of its parent's contract — overriding to throw, no-op, or narrow the result — when client code type-checks or switches on concrete subtypes, when asked whether an inheritance hierarchy or "is-a" relationship is sound, or when tests for the base contract need skips for a subtype, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question. Subtypes must be substitutable for their base type without surprising callers. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-liskov-substitution))
- **principle-local-autonomy**: Apply when reversible local work can proceed without a permission pause. Keep agents autonomous inside the workspace while reserving external communication and consequential actions for the user. ([petalas/skills](https://github.com/petalas/skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/principle-local-autonomy/skills/principle-local-autonomy) · ★1)
- **principle-long-term**: Changes built to last, so they are done once. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-long-term))
- **principle-look-beyond-the-diff**: Apply before calling any change complete. The bugs reviewers find are usually outside the changed lines: other callers, other readers of the same data, test mocks of the old behavior, docs that state the old value, and texts that describe the same fact. Search the whole tree for them. ([Nikshay1/nystack](https://github.com/Nikshay1/nystack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-look-beyond-the-diff))
- **principle-make-concurrency-explicit**: Apply when requests, jobs, callbacks, processes, or parallel agents can overlap while using mutable state or effects. Trace interleavings, prefer independent state where valid, and enforce shared invariants at the appropriate atomic boundary. ([alexph/brunel](https://github.com/alexph/brunel/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-make-concurrency-explicit))
- **principle-make-impossible-states-unrepresentable**: Apply when designing types or state, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption: model the domain so invalid combinations cannot be built (unions, state machines, branded primitives, exhaustive matches, types derived from the schema). ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-impossible-states-unrepresentable) · ★1)
- **principle-make-it-smart**: Applies when a whats-going-on stage proposes a SMART question for the primary or asks about a missing SMART letter. Propose only what the input grounds. ([unfriction-ai/whats-going-on](https://github.com/unfriction-ai/whats-going-on/tree/HEAD/plugins/whats-going-on/skills/whats-going-on/principle-make-it-smart) · 2 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([elizaOS/eliza](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★20k · 11k installs · 1.1k copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([cursor/plugins](https://github.com/cursor/plugins) · ★17k · 5.4k installs)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([backnotprop/pstack](https://github.com/backnotprop/pstack) · ★4.5k · 11k installs)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([michael-denyer/pstack-claude](https://github.com/michael-denyer/pstack-claude) · ★4.5k · 1k installs)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Use when designing commands, setup or install flows, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that might be retried or restarted. Every run, including one following a crash at any step, must end in the same correct state. ([hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins](https://github.com/hashgraph-online/awesome-codex-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/requie/endostack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★1.6k · 5 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Design repeated execution to converge on the same correct end state rather than multiplying side effects. Use when for commands, lifecycle steps, retries, loops, or crash recovery. ([sreichholf/dreamDroid](https://github.com/sreichholf/dreamDroid/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★117 · 4 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. ([jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills](https://github.com/jeremybrasher/grokbot-skills/tree/HEAD/collections/pstack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★38)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: WHEN commands or lifecycle operations can repeat or resume after interruption; NOT for assuming local process IDs prove distributed ownership; reconciles partial state to the intended outcome. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★37)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands or steps that may rerun. ([Cloeille/pstack](https://github.com/Cloeille/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★28 · 3 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, scrapers, cron collectors, migrations, or lifecycle scripts. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs or retries. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/tree/HEAD/dist/codex/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★20 · 7 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Design commands, jobs and external effects to recover safely from repeated execution or interruption. ([sjdonado/dotfiles](https://github.com/sjdonado/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/agents/modes/sjdonado-mode/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★16)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: pstack, typed-only. Load only when the user names this skill or a loaded pstack skill routes to it. Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. Converge to the same correct end state no matter where a prior run stopped. ([itsvedantkumar/vstack](https://github.com/itsvedantkumar/vstack/tree/HEAD/claude/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★5 · 3 copies)

- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Make Operations Idempotent ([PickleBoxer/pstack](https://github.com/PickleBoxer/pstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★4)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Use when implementing retries, offline queues, sync, installers or operations that may run repeatedly. ([wrestle-R/Mbappe-Special](https://github.com/wrestle-R/Mbappe-Special/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★3)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Make operations idempotent. Apply when you design commands, lifecycle steps or processing loops that run among crashes, restarts and retries. ([erickb336/sage-stack](https://github.com/erickb336/sage-stack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sage-codex/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★2 · 2 copies)

- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. ([lovrozagar/grunt](https://github.com/lovrozagar/grunt/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★2 · 20 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when writing anything that mutates state and can be retried, re-run or interrupted, such as a migration, a CLI command, a job, a mail send, a webhook handler or a deploy step. It must reach the same end state no matter how often it runs or where the last run stopped. ([patryksztuczka/boberstack](https://github.com/patryksztuczka/boberstack/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Use when writing or reviewing a command, migration, job, or setup script that may crash, restart, retry, or run twice ("is this retry-safe", "what if this runs twice"). Make it converge to the same end state whatever a prior run left. ([ravnhq/ravn-core](https://github.com/ravnhq/ravn-core/tree/HEAD/plugins/agent/ravn-core/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★2)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: 크래시, 재시작, 재시도 속에서 실행되는 명령, 수명 주기 단계, 처리 루프를 설계할 때 적용한다. 앞선 실행이 일부만 진행됐더라도 같은 최종 상태로 수렴하게 한다. ([yjb94/jbstack](https://github.com/yjb94/jbstack/tree/HEAD/core/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★2)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Steps that run amid crashes and retries. Same end state however prior runs ended. ([blackviperxiii-ui/GrokHub](https://github.com/blackviperxiii-ui/GrokHub/tree/HEAD/crates/grokhub-agent/assets/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when writing anything that changes state and can be retried, restarted or crash halfway (commands, migrations, installers, job loops): make every run converge to the same end state. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★1)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state. ([danieltsam/pstack-opencode](https://github.com/danieltsam/pstack-opencode/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · ★1)

- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle operations, or processing loops that face crashes, restarts, and retries. Reconcile actual state with the goal so repeated and partial runs converge to the same result. Use when explicitly invoked as @principle-make-operations-idempotent. ([kyeshmz/jiro](https://github.com/kyeshmz/jiro/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply to operations that may be retried after partial execution. Reconcile current state toward the declared result without duplicating effects. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · 2 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: 충돌, 재시작, 재시도 중에 실행되는 명령, 라이프사이클 단계 또는 처리 루프를 설계할 때 적용. 부분 이전 실행에 관계없이 동일한 최종 상태로 수렴. ([lee-chance/ai-skills-ko](https://github.com/lee-chance/ai-skills-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack-ko/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Design repeatable operations to converge safely when retried. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: 在设计会在崩溃、重启和重试中运行的命令、生命周期步骤或处理循环时应用。无论先前运行是否只完成了一部分，都收敛到同一个最终状态。 ([levin-design/lstack](https://github.com/levin-design/lstack/tree/HEAD/copilot/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Nội quy khi thiết kế lệnh, bước vòng đời, vòng xử lý có thể crash/restart/retry: chạy lại bao nhiêu lần cũng về cùng một trạng thái cuối. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Only use when poteto-mode is active for this task. Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([mallochio/pstack-ports](https://github.com/mallochio/pstack-ports/tree/HEAD/prime-agent/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · 2 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Converge to the same end state regardless of what a previous partial run left behind. Use when writing scripts, migrations, infrastructure code or any operation that may be rerun. ([mcgeerdev/.dotfiles](https://github.com/mcgeerdev/.dotfiles/tree/HEAD/wow/.omp/agent/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Architecture. Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. ([Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config](https://github.com/Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config/tree/HEAD/dot_claude/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Designs commands, lifecycle steps, and loops to reach the same end state after crashes, restarts, and retries. ([NikitaKurpas/agents](https://github.com/NikitaKurpas/agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/standards/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Use when designing commands, lifecycle steps or processing loops that face crashes, restarts and retries: make every rerun converge to the same end state from any partial prior run. ([PatrickFanella/patstack](https://github.com/PatrickFanella/patstack/tree/HEAD/library/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent) · 2 copies)
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when writing an operation that mutates state: commands, lifecycle hooks, file writers, scheduled jobs, state synchronizers. Derive each step from the state that exists, apply only the difference, leave markers that a crash does not confuse. ([PlagueFPS/skills](https://github.com/PlagueFPS/skills/tree/HEAD/pstack/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: 适用于设计会在崩溃、重启和重试之间运行的命令、生命周期步骤或处理循环时。不管上一次跑到哪，都收敛到同一终态。 ([Praying/pstack-zh](https://github.com/Praying/pstack-zh/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Stosuj przy mutacji, imporcie, retry i pętli, która może paść w połowie. Ten sam stan końcowy niezależnie od częściowego przebiegu. EN - make operations idempotent, safe retry. ([Prezentytu/fiziyo-admin-portal](https://github.com/Prezentytu/fiziyo-admin-portal/tree/HEAD/.ai/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: 충돌, 재시작, 재시도 속에서 도는 명령, 수명 주기 단계, 처리 루프를 설계할 때 적용한다. 이전 실행이 어디까지 됐든 같은 최종 상태로 수렴하게 한다. ([rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko](https://github.com/rimoir/ref-agent-plugins-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Design state-mutating operations to converge on the same end state no matter how many times they run or where a prior run died. Use when writing or reviewing migrations, seeds, key rotation, epoch rollover, or any lifecycle step that can be retried. ([Sapiev/Veya](https://github.com/Sapiev/Veya/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: クラッシュ、再起動、リトライのなかで実行されるコマンド、ライフサイクルのステップ、処理ループを設計するときに適用する。以前の実行が途中で終わっていても、同じ最終状態に収束させる。 ([seiei-sogen/agent-skills](https://github.com/seiei-sogen/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pstack-skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: For the Box kitchen project only. Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([shouteck/omakase-box](https://github.com/shouteck/omakase-box/tree/HEAD/.devin/plugins/box-pstack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. Explicitly invoke this pstack entry as $principle-make-operations-idempotent. ([starrybamboo/agent-skills](https://github.com/starrybamboo/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/harness/codex/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Design operations so they converge to the correct state regardless of how many times they run or where they start from. Every state-mutating operation should answer: "What happens if this runs twice? What happens if the previous run crashed halfway?" ([thnkbig/skills](https://github.com/thnkbig/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: Apply when designing jobs, commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs. ([YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles](https://github.com/YuukiFST/agent-dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent**: 设计会在崩溃、重启和重试中运行的命令、生命周期步骤或处理循环时使用。无论之前的运行完成了多少，都收敛到同一个终态。 ([zheyi420/Dev-Wiki](https://github.com/zheyi420/Dev-Wiki/tree/HEAD/AI/Agent/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent))
- **principle-make-operations-idempotent-pi**: Pi adaptation: Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. - **principle-make-structure-visible**: Apply when creating or reviewing code, tests, documentation, or explanations. Make logical grouping, sequence, and branches visible to the next reader. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-make-structure-visible))
- **principle-makefile-first**: Prefer established Makefile targets over raw commands when a project provides them. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-makefile-first))
- **principle-manage-idle-resumption**: Apply when a session may idle for a multi-hour or multi-day gap: polling loops, human-in-the-loop waits, overnight runs, babysit/watch/retry work. Compact or restart deliberately before resuming instead of letting the prompt cache lapse. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-manage-idle-resumption) · ★2)
- **principle-match-delivery-to-task**: Apply when planning deliverables, writing documentation, or deciding task completion. Match delivery to investigation, implementation, or explicitly requested standalone research outcomes. ([lexedwards/daedalus](https://github.com/lexedwards/daedalus/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-match-delivery-to-task))
- **principle-measure-query-cost**: Apply before claiming a SQL or ORM query change is faster or cheaper. Run EXPLAIN ANALYZE (or the ORM's query count) before and after on the same input - reasoning like 'fewer joins' or 'one subquery instead of three' isn't proof on its own. ([sutanto62/nalgene](https://github.com/sutanto62/nalgene/tree/HEAD/nalgene-mode/skills/principle-measure-query-cost) · ★4)
- **principle-measure-variation**: Apply when results vary: LLM and agent outputs, distributed timing, unit-to-unit differences in hardware, environmental conditions, or a production process. Run enough trials or samples. Report rates and spreads with their sample size. Do not decide from a single run or a single unit. ([Jacky97s/qstack](https://github.com/Jacky97s/qstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-measure-variation) · ★1)
- **principle-mechanism-not-policy**: Apply when designing a tool, library or platform that other projects build on and you are adding a restriction, a default, a special-case path or a feature request to it. ([kapetan-io/dispatch](https://github.com/kapetan-io/dispatch/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-mechanism-not-policy))
- **principle-mechanization-ladder**: Core. Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction or correction a second time. ([Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config](https://github.com/Nagato-Yuzuru/eureka-config/tree/HEAD/dot_claude/skills/principle-mechanization-ladder))
- **principle-metadata-is-code**: Apply when touching flows, validation rules, objects, permissions, or any Salesforce metadata. Metadata is code: it lives in version control, moves through source-driven deployment, and gets review, tests, and blast-radius checks like Apex. Org-based development is an anti-pattern. ([krika2810/sfstack](https://github.com/krika2810/sfstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-metadata-is-code) · ★1)

- **principle-minimal-comments**: Load before writing, editing or reviewing code in any language, whether or not comments come up, and hold to it over the comment style of the surrounding code. Code carries no comments by default: make it explain itself by renaming, extracting or rewriting. Only a workaround that reads as a mistake without one keeps a comment, written for a reader seeing the final version cold. ([huntie/skills](https://github.com/huntie/skills/tree/HEAD/principle-minimal-comments))

- **principle-minimize-context**: Build bounded worker or verifier context when available source material contains broad history, unrelated files, or large command output. ([kriscard/Kriscard-Stack](https://github.com/kriscard/Kriscard-Stack/tree/HEAD/skills/dev/principle-minimize-context))

- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Reduces the layers a reader traces and the hidden or mutable state they hold in their head. Use when shaping or reviewing code that is hard to trace, and when judging whether a refactor made code simpler. ([jiyeol-lee/dotfiles](https://github.com/jiyeol-lee/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.opencode/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Make code quicker to trace. Use when code is hard to follow: count the layers between question and answer and the hidden state a reader must hold, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope. ([kausthubh-coder/skills](https://github.com/kausthubh-coder/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Cut layers and hidden state a reader must hold. Used by kevbot when code is hard to trace. ([kevherro/kevbot](https://github.com/kevherro/kevbot/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: When you review or shape code that is hard to trace, count the layers a reader must follow and the state they must hold, then collapse wrappers and shrink mutable scope. ([kpeez/agent-toolbox](https://github.com/kpeez/agent-toolbox/tree/HEAD/plugins/swe/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Compatibility entry point for Jiro's Minimize Reader Load skill. Use when reviewing tracing layers or hidden and mutable state. ([kyeshmz/jiro](https://github.com/kyeshmz/jiro/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Apply when code is difficult to trace or requires hidden context. Minimize indirection and the mutable state a maintainer must remember. ([lachieh/skills](https://github.com/lachieh/skills/tree/HEAD/.apm/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load) · 2 copies)
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: 추적하기 어려운 코드를 검토하거나 형성할 때 적용. 질문과 답변 사이의 레이어와 독자의 머릿속 숨겨진 상태를 세고; 하나의 호출자 래퍼를 접고 가변 범위를 줄임. ([lee-chance/ai-skills-ko](https://github.com/lee-chance/ai-skills-ko/tree/HEAD/pstack-ko/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Keep intent and control flow easy to understand by reducing unnecessary indirection and repetition. ([leehosanganson/dotfiles](https://github.com/leehosanganson/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/ai/.config/ai/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: 在审查或塑造难以追踪的代码时应用。数一数问题与答案之间的层数，以及读者脑中要记住的隐藏状态；折叠只有一个调用方的包装器，缩小可变作用域。 ([levin-design/lstack](https://github.com/levin-design/lstack/tree/HEAD/copilot/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))
- **principle-minimize-reader-load**: Nội quy khi review hoặc sửa code khó lần theo: đếm số lớp từ câu hỏi tới câu trả lời và trạng thái người đọc phải nhớ; gộp wrapper chỉ có một nơi gọi, thu hẹp phạm vi biến đổi. ([lichihouse/claude-plugins](https://github.com/lichihouse/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load))

- **principle-modules-should-be-deep**: Use when designing, sketching, or reviewing a module, class, API, or abstraction — including a quick sketch of a public interface in a few lines — or when asked whether a layer, wrapper, or interface is worth keeping, even for a short snippet or a conceptual question, and even if the user does not mention depth, abstraction, or interfaces. Favors a simple interface hiding substantial functionality over shallow or pass-through code. ([dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness](https://github.com/dmenezesgabriel/agent-harness/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-modules-should-be-deep))
- **principle-monitoring**: 監視とアラートを設計、レビューするときの原則。何を見るか、通知の絞り方と送り先、監視そのものの守り方を扱う。AWS と Sentry に固有の観点も含む。 ([sasamuku/dotfiles](https://github.com/sasamuku/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-monitoring) · ★10)
- **principle-motion-decision**: Decide whether a UI element should animate at all, and if so its purpose, easing, and duration. Use before writing any animation, and when reviewing one. ([NickDabizaz/skills](https://github.com/NickDabizaz/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-motion-decision))
- **principle-name-events-not-setters**: Apply when naming actions, messages, callbacks or state transitions: name the domain fact that happened (submitted, item_added), not the field it sets. ([cevr/dotfiles](https://github.com/cevr/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-name-events-not-setters) · ★1)
- **principle-name-for-the-reader**: Choose NeoHaskell module, type, and operation names that a junior TypeScript developer can understand without knowing GHC implementation terms. ([neohaskell/NeoHaskell](https://github.com/neohaskell/NeoHaskell/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/principle-name-for-the-reader) · ★348)
- **principle-name-the-destination**: Apply before decomposing work into tickets, phases, or a map. State what done looks like before you break anything into parts. ([mattlindell/photon-plugins](https://github.com/mattlindell/photon-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/ph-plan/skills/principles/principle-name-the-destination))
- **principle-name-the-estimand**: Apply before any statistical test, effect estimate, confidence interval, regression, or clustering: A/B tests, promotion or campaign effects, customer segments, churn or lifetime value. Name the estimand, the unit, the comparison, and the assumptions the method needs, pick the method from them, and report an effect size with its uncertainty instead of a bare p-value. ([hidenobunagai/astack](https://github.com/hidenobunagai/astack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-name-the-estimand) · ★1)
- **principle-name-the-scorer**: Apply when a product has two (or more) scorers that both emit weights, priorities, or rankings — especially hand-authored theme/report priorities beside learned historical-case tag weights. Name which scorer produced which number; never imply one sets the other unless wiring is explicit. ([EdbertChan/catstack](https://github.com/EdbertChan/catstack/tree/HEAD/corpus/skills/principle-name-the-scorer) · ★2)
- **principle-name-the-tradeoffs**: Use when a real choice changes complexity, risk, cost, or reversibility, or when one option is presented for a decision that has others. ([ShravanSunder/sunstack](https://github.com/ShravanSunder/sunstack/tree/HEAD/plugins/sunstack-dev/skills/principle-name-the-tradeoffs))
- **principle-named-constraints**: List and name every delivery constraint — budget, spend authority, git authority, tracker access, time, state trust — before starting work, because an unnamed constraint gets violated and a named one chooses the path. Apply before executing any task whose environment may restrict the obvious path. ([vraj-ai/skills](https://github.com/vraj-ai/skills/tree/HEAD/principles/principle-named-constraints) · ★3)
- **principle-named-shape**: Name the data and its invariant before writing logic. Used by kevbot before implementation. ([kevherro/kevbot](https://github.com/kevherro/kevbot/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-named-shape))
- **principle-names-reveal-intent**: Apply when naming or renaming a variable, function, type, module, or file, or when a reviewer has to ask what a name means. Pick the name that answers why it exists, what it does, and how it is used, so no comment has to. ([thojou/tjstack](https://github.com/thojou/tjstack/tree/HEAD/tjstack/skills/principles/principle-names-reveal-intent))
- **principle-naming**: Names that say what a thing is in the domain. ([Nusnus/nustack](https://github.com/Nusnus/nustack/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/principle-naming))
- **principle-naming**: Use when naming or renaming anything another reader will meet later: a file, a directory, a report or document, a scratch artifact, a variable, function, type, constant, branch, or commit subject. Fires before writing any new file, whenever a name is about to carry a generic stem, a number, a date, a version word, an acronym, or a codename, and when reviewing names in a diff. Exists to stop one failure: names that only decode for someone who was in the session. ([Uraxii/dotai](https://github.com/Uraxii/dotai/tree/HEAD/plugins/pstack/skills/principle-naming))
- **principle-narrow-state-lifetime**: WHEN mutable state outlives or outreaches the work that needs it, such as module globals, long-lived fields or reset hooks; NOT concurrent writers sharing one invariant, or tracing layers; scopes each value to its use with explicit inputs. ([mintuz/skills](https://github.com/mintuz/skills/tree/HEAD/src/principle/skills/principle-narrow-state-lifetime) · ★37)
- **principle-narrowest-seam**: Apply when you choose where a test goes, or review a test. Start from the behavior, then test it at the narrowest seam that still holds the whole behavior. ([mightymoose/development-tooling](https://github.com/mightymoose/development-tooling/tree/HEAD/mm-mode/skills/principle-narrowest-seam))
- **principle-never-block-on-human**: Apply when tempted to ask whether to take a reversible action that the task already authorizes. ([ImAno177/cstack](https://github.com/ImAno177/cstack/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-human))
- **principle-never-block-on-reversible-work**: Apply when tempted to ask permission for reversible work. Proceed and present the result. Does not apply to irreversible actions. ([daryl-tg/deez-skills](https://github.com/daryl-tg/deez-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/principle-never-block-on-reversible-work))

Observe required inputs first; HOLD when they are missing rather than guessing. - **principle-never-block-on-the-human**: pstack, typed-only. Load only when the user names this skill or a loaded pstack skill routes to it. ([smasato/dotfiles](https://github.com/smasato/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/dot_agents/skills/principle-never-block-on-the-human) · ★9)
